# Backlog — the default home for unsprinted task briefs

**This is not a sprint.** It is the standing board for work that has been scoped into a brief but not
yet scheduled into one. A brief written with no sprint named lands here, and stays here until someone
pulls it into a sprint.

**⚠️ The filename is deliberately `backlog.md`, NOT `sprint-backlog.md`.** `/fkit-status` resolves the
active sprint by each plan's **identity**, with **no pattern on the filename**: this board's identity
is `Backlog`, which is **never eligible**, so the default status run ignores it no matter what the
file is called
([ADR-041 §§2–3](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-041-the-active-sprint-is-selected-by-resolved-identity-not-by-filename-glob.md)).
**That exclusion is stronger than the filename rule it replaced** — renaming this file could not make
it the active sprint. The name is kept for a different reason: its href is written into every
`➡️ Moved to [Backlog](backlog.md)` marker in the repo. **Do not "normalize" this name.**

## How work moves on and off this board

- **On:** `/fkit-task-brief` with no sprint named files the brief with `## Sprint: Backlog` and adds a
  row here (creating this file if it is absent).
- **Off:** when the **producer** pulls a task into a sprint, **three** edits, all mandatory: add the
  row to that sprint plan; flip the row here to `➡️ Moved to [Sprint N](sprint-N.md) — priority M`
  (the canonical marker — `M` is the priority the task gets in Sprint N, and the `— priority M`
  component is **not** optional here); **and update the brief's own `## Sprint` to `Sprint N`.**
  The row here is **not deleted** — a deleted row loses the pointer to where the work went.
  > **⚠️ Skip the brief-side update and the row never goes away.** Drift rule 2 compares the `Moved`
  > target against the brief's `## Sprint`; a mismatch is `drift disagreement`, and a drifted row
  > always renders. Every pulled task would leave a permanent drifted row on this board.
- **Back on, from a sprint (the reverse move):** when the **producer** de-scopes a task out of a sprint
  and back onto this board, **five** edits, all mandatory:
  1. Flip the **sprint plan's** row to `➡️ Moved to [Backlog](backlog.md)` — the canonical marker from
     [`task-status-vocabulary.md`](../knowledge-base/conventions/task-status-vocabulary.md). **No
     `— priority M` suffix**: this board is unranked, so there is no destination rank to name. The href
     is **relative to the file holding the row** — `backlog.md` from `ai-agents/sprints/`, and it must
     become `../backlog.md` when that sprint plan is later archived under `sprints/done/`.
     **The sprint row is not deleted, and its own `P<n>` Priority cell is left alone** — a closed-out
     row is frozen history, and the number stays readable there.
  2. Add a row **here**, Status `🔲 Backlog`, Priority cell `—` (see *Priority*, below).
  3. **Update the brief's own `## Sprint` field to `Backlog`.**
  4. **Update the brief's own `## Status` field to `🔲 Backlog`** — a task de-scoped while it read
     `🔄 In progress` or `🚧 Blocked` is not in progress any more; it is unscheduled.
  5. **Update the brief's own `## Priority` field to `Unscheduled`** — the rank is *surrendered*, not
     parked. It stays recoverable by reading the sprint row it came from.
  > **⚠️ Skip edit 3 and the row never goes away** — same trap as **Off:** above, in the other
  > direction. Drift rule 2 compares the `Moved` target (`Backlog`) against the brief's `## Sprint`; a
  > mismatch is `drift disagreement`, and a drifted row always renders.
  >
  > **⚠️ Skip edit 4 and the NEW row here never goes away either** — the same trap, one field over.
  > This board cross-checks its own row against the brief's `## Status` (rule 1 is deliberately
  > disabled here), so a brief left reading `🔄 In progress` renders `waiting on owner` permanently.
  > Verified 2026-08-03 on a post-move fixture built by following these steps exactly:
  > `drift disagreement 0001 plan="🔲 Backlog" brief="🔄 In progress" brief_sprint="Backlog"
  > location="backlog/"`.
  >
  > **⚠️ Edit 5 is UNENFORCED — nothing in the repo checks it.** `dashboard.sh` reads a brief's
  > `Status`, `Sprint` and `Owner`; it never reads `## Priority`. A brief left reading `## Priority:
  > 152` on this unranked board is invisible to every control we have, so it survives only by being
  > done deliberately.
  >
  > **⚠️ Five here, three under Off:, and that is not an inconsistency.** The forward move needs no
  > `## Status` edit at all — a brief being pulled already reads `🔲 Backlog`, and so does the row it
  > gains in the sprint plan — and it folds its `## Priority` edit into its own step 3 as the
  > parenthetical *"(and give `## Priority` the real number)"* rather than numbering it.
- **Closed here:** a backlog task can be completed or cancelled without ever joining a sprint.
  `/fkit-task-done` / `/fkit-task-cancelled` already sweep `ai-agents/sprints/` recursively, so they
  find and flip rows in this file unchanged.

## Priority

**The Priority column reads `—` by design: this board is unranked.** Ranking is what a sprint is for.
A number here would be a commitment nobody made — and the briefs themselves record `## Priority:
Unscheduled` to match (some add a free-text qualifier after it, e.g.
`Unscheduled — high-value (…)`; that is the brief's own note and **never** becomes a number in this
column). If a task needs a rank, that is the signal to pull it into a sprint, not to number it here.

## Addendum — the owner-ruled fold of 2026-08-11 (`0271` grows from three items to five)

**Authority:** owner ruling 2026-08-11, given live via `AskUserQuestion` in a `fkit lead` session,
verbatim option label **"Fold into 0271"**. Source of truth for the wording is
[`0265`](../tasks/done/0265-implement-adr-041s-dashboard-half-the-backlog-identity-token-and-the-resolve-identity-interface/brief.md)'s
`review.md`, *Accepted residuals*.

Two residuals from `0265`'s review were folded into the existing `0271` brief rather than filed as a
new task. They are the same shape as its first three items — **a correct behavior with no test that
reds when it is undone** — and cover `0265`'s `select-active` half:

| Added item | Was | What is unpinned |
|---|---|---|
| **4** | `0265` R4 / residual **A1** | The `[ -f ]` no-match guard on the candidate glob — **removing it leaves the suite green (141/141)**. When it fires (empty `sprints/`) the result is a phantom candidate record and a `head:` stderr line; **selection stays correct**. ⚠️ The *dangerous* half, the `set +f` wrapper, **is already pinned** — removing it reds **7** tests. Do not conflate them. |
| **5** | `0265` R7 / residual **A2** | Two comment claims no test exercises: (a) ADR-041 §1.1's `sprints/done/` exclusion, and (b) *"a plan file literally named `identity` still renders as a board"*. Both verified correct by direct probe in `0265`; neither reds if undone. |

⛔ **Ruled OUT of the fold, and not to be pulled in later without a new ruling:** `0265`'s **R1** (false
locale guard) and **R5** (unreadable candidate resolving to a wrong identity) — both **fixed and
red-proved inside `0265`**, and R1 additionally recorded by the reviewer as outside `0271`'s scope;
`0265` residual **A3/R6** (leading-dash path noise — **accepted as-is**, fails safe); and residual
**A4** (newline/TAB in a basename — **accepted as-is, code unchanged**, now disclosed as a limit).
**The task grew by exactly two items.**

⚠️ **The folder name still says "three"** —
`0271-pin-the-three-unpinned-behaviors-in-the-sprint-identity-grammar`. Renaming it is a task-file
move, which only the mover skills may perform, so the in-file H1 was updated and the divergence
flagged in the brief instead. **The folder name is dated, not wrong.**
